Social and lifestyle history covers occupation, activity level, and habits, while aggravating and relieving factors address what changes current symptoms rather than past events.
  244. The correct answer is C (The supraspinatus tendon against the anterior acromion): Passive forward flexion with internal rotation compresses the supraspinatus tendon against the anterior edge of the acromion, reproducing impingement pain. The glenoid labrum is assessed with different provocation and compression maneuvers rather than this passive flexion motion. The axillary nerve and subscapularis tendon have their own distinct assessment tests that do not rely on this specific passive forward flexion technique.
  245. The correct answer is A (Loose clothing can obscure bony landmarks and true body alignment): since visualizing landmarks and contours clearly is essential for an accurate postural assessment. This preference is a clinical accuracy consideration rather than a dress code mandate. Comfort level with clothing type varies by individual and is not the primary clinical reason, and clothing choice has no effect on the client's physical balance during observation.
  246. The correct answer is D (Gluteus medius tendon): This muscle is the primary hip abductor, and resisted abduction reproducing lateral hip pain strongly implicates its tendon near the greater trochanter. The adductor longus produces hip adduction rather than abduction, so it would not be loaded by this specific resisted test. Iliopsoas and piriformis are primarily hip flexor and external rotator structures respectively, not prime abductors tested by this maneuver.
  247. The correct answer is C (Specific functional movements that provoke the presenting symptoms): since identifying that an inclined or loaded movement like stairs provokes symptoms while level walking does not helps narrow the likely involved structures and mechanism. This detail says nothing about footwear preference on its own. It also does not reveal medication dosage or family history, both of which are separate categories of the health history.
  248. The correct answer is A (Stop the movement immediately and avoid pushing into painful range further): Respecting acute pain signals during screening prevents unnecessary tissue irritation and keeps the assessment safe for the client. Continuing through severe pain or applying overpressure risks aggravating the underlying issue rather than gathering useful information. Moving directly to a resisted test in the same painful direction is not an appropriate immediate response to acute pain during screening.
  249. The correct answer is A (A measurable, therapist-observed palpation finding): since this describes tissue quality assessed directly by the therapist's hands, appropriate for the objective section. It is not a subjective report, which would instead capture the client's own words about their experience. This entry is also not a treatment goal or an imaging-confirmed diagnosis, both of which belong elsewhere in clinical documentation.
  250. The correct answer is D (Insufficient knee or hip flexion needed to clear the foot): When a client cannot adequately flex the knee or hip to shorten the limb during swing, circumduction becomes a compensatory strategy to clear the foot off the ground. Excessive dorsiflexion range would not create a clearance problem requiring this compensation. Overactive adductors or excessive hip flexor flexibility do not typically produce this particular circular swing compensation pattern.

Practice Test 2 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is D (When did you first notice this problem and what were you doing at the time): This question establishes onset and possible mechanism, forming a foundational piece of the presenting complaint history. Asking whether the client believes surgery is needed asks for a diagnostic opinion rather than gathering history. Technique preference and prior therapist history are secondary details that do not establish the onset or mechanism of the current complaint.
  2. The correct answer is C (The test is still considered positive since symptoms were reproduced): since a positive result depends on reproducing the characteristic symptoms, not on reaching the maximum time allowed. There is no requirement to repeat the test to the full duration once symptoms have already appeared. Faster symptom reproduction does not make the test negative, and clinical special tests are interpreted based on the reproduced signs and symptoms rather than requiring imaging confirmation.
  3. The correct answer is C (The subtalar joint and medial longitudinal arch): Excessive pronation directly reflects the position of the subtalar joint and the flattening of the medial arch, both foot and ankle region findings. The glenohumeral and sternoclavicular joints are shoulder girdle structures unrelated to foot pronation. The atlanto-occipital joint is located at the upper cervical spine and has no direct relationship to foot alignment findings.
  4. The correct answer is B (Repetitive postures or loads that may contribute to the presenting condition): since daily activity and occupational demands often reveal repetitive stresses or postures linked to the client's symptoms. Wage information and insurance qualification are administrative details unrelated to clinical reasoning about the condition. Oil preference is a treatment logistics detail, not a reason for asking about occupational activity.
  5. The correct answer is A (Using more than one test for the same structure increases the assessment's diagnostic confidence): since combining tests that target a similar suspected structure through different mechanisms helps build a more reliable clinical picture. Neither test is invalid on its own, and using both is not about discarding one in favor of the other. No combination of special tests guarantees complete diagnostic certainty, and both tests do share overlapping relevance to shoulder impingement assessment.
  6. The correct answer is C (Likely tightness of the hamstring muscle group rather than nerve root tension): Discomfort appearing only at the extreme end range without radiating symptoms is more consistent with simple hamstring tightness than true neural tension. True nerve root tension classically reproduces radiating leg symptoms within the mid-range of hip flexion rather than only at the extreme end. This finding alone is not conclusive for disc herniation, sciatic compression, or sacroiliac dysfunction, all of which require additional corroborating findings.
  7. The correct answer is D (A functional limitation specific to a particular range of the shoulder): Describing which activities are difficult versus which remain unaffected narrows the likely range of motion or structure involved in the complaint. This subjective functional detail does not itself provide a specific manual muscle strength grade, which requires hands-on testing. It also does not reveal surgical history or dictate a specific treatment technique on its own.
  8. The correct answer is C (Respect the client's hesitation and either explain further or modify the approach): since ongoing consent requires responding to client concerns rather than proceeding regardless. Standard protocol does not override the client's right to decline or ask for modification. Neither a signed waiver nor simple documentation of hesitation justifies proceeding with a technique the client has not clearly agreed to.
  9. The correct answer is C (The gastrocnemius muscle at its junction with the Achilles tendon): This specific myotendinous junction is a common site of acute strain injury in the calf, often called a tennis leg injury. The peroneus longus and tibialis posterior tendons run along the lateral and medial lower leg respectively, distinct from the posterior calf myotendinous junction. The flexor hallucis longus lies deep in the posterior compartment and is not the classic site associated with this presentation.
  10. The correct answer is A (Whether the current treatment approach should continue or be adjusted): since tracking documented progress across sessions directly informs clinical decision making about continuing or modifying the plan. Billing amounts and appointment scheduling preferences are administrative matters unrelated to interpreting a progress trend. Lotion brand selection is a treatment logistics choice, not something informed by range of motion progress notes.
  11. The correct answer is D (To gauge the immediate effect of the treatment on the client's symptoms): Comparing pre and post treatment pain ratings provides a quick measure of whether the session produced a noticeable change in the client's symptoms. This comparison does not reveal the underlying cause of the original condition, which requires broader assessment findings. It also has no connection to insurance eligibility or fee calculation for future appointments.
  12. The correct answer is C (A cervical compression or foraminal closing test for nerve root involvement): This position narrows the intervertebral foramen on the tested side, and reproduced arm tingling suggests nerve root compression consistent with a positive foraminal closing test. A vertebral artery test instead uses sustained rotation and extension with attention to visual or vertigo symptoms, not arm tingling. Temporomandibular and ligament instability tests use entirely different positions and provocation methods unrelated to this specific combined extension and lateral flexion maneuver.
  13. The correct answer is B (An insidious or gradual onset): the term specifically describing symptoms developing slowly over time without an identifiable triggering event. A traumatic or acute onset instead describes symptoms beginning suddenly following a specific incident, which contradicts the gradual, event-free presentation described. Referred onset describes the location relationship of pain to its source, not the timing pattern of when symptoms first appeared.
  14. The correct answer is A (These changes can be red flags requiring urgent medical referral): Bowel or bladder changes combined with certain spinal symptoms can signal a serious condition such as cauda equina syndrome, requiring immediate referral rather than routine assessment. This screening question has no bearing on oil selection or treatment duration preferences. It is relevant across client populations and is not restricted to pediatric clients specifically.
  15. The correct answer is A (Contractile tissue such as a muscle or tendon): since pain reproduced specifically with resisted contraction while passive and active motion remain pain free strongly implicates the contractile unit rather than surrounding non-contractile structures. Inert tissues like ligaments are more typically stressed and provoked by passive stretch rather than active muscular contraction. Osseous and vascular tissue involvement would generally present with different signs not isolated to resisted muscular testing alone.
  16. The correct answer is A (Generalized pain, deconditioning, or cautious guarding): Bilateral shortened steps and reduced speed without a specific asymmetric deviation often reflect a general protective or deconditioned gait pattern rather than one isolated structural cause. A unilateral ankle sprain or single localized strain would more typically produce an asymmetric limp rather than a symmetric bilateral slowing. A structural leg length discrepancy usually produces a specific limb-length related deviation rather than this general bilateral pattern.
  17. The correct answer is B (Consider the overall clinical picture and possibly repeat or supplement with other tests): Clinical reasoning requires weighing all gathered information together rather than relying on a single inconsistent finding in isolation. Discarding the health history entirely in favor of one test result oversimplifies the clinical reasoning process. Automatically blaming technique error or ignoring the inconsistency altogether both bypass the careful reasoning needed to resolve conflicting assessment data.
  18. The correct answer is B (A cervical postural asymmetry or possible torticollis pattern): since a resting head tilt and rotation combination is a recognized cervical postural finding that may reflect muscular imbalance or a torticollis type presentation. This is not considered typical or expected in the general population as a normal variant. A lumbar spine or foot and ankle finding would not directly explain a head position asymmetry observed at the cervical region.
  19. The correct answer is B (To protect the public interest by governing regulated health professions): the RHPA establishes a framework of colleges whose overriding duty is public protection rather than promoting members' interests. Option A confuses a college's role with that of a professional association. Option C is unrelated to the RHPA's regulatory purpose. Option D is incorrect because Ontario regulates professions provincially, not nationally.
  20. Option B does not exist as a shared procedural document. Option C concerns hospitals, not individual professional regulation. Option D relates to corporate structures, not complaint or discipline procedures.
  21. Option A is a fabricated title with no legal standing. Option B describes a type of regulation rather than a profession-specific statute. Option D does not correspond to any actual Ontario legislation governing massage therapy.
  22.   23.   29.   30. The correct answer is C (Members must recognize and address conditions that could impair their safe practice): members have an ongoing duty to be aware of and manage any condition, physical or mental, that could compromise their ability to practise safely. Option A incorrectly limits fitness assessment to a single point in time. Option B wrongly ties fitness concerns to age rather than actual capability. Option D improperly shifts a personal professional responsibility entirely onto an employer.

Option C confuses quality assurance with promotional activity for the profession.

Option D mischaracterizes the review as a financial sanction rather than an evaluative process.

  91. The correct answer is A (Supraspinatus): this rotator cuff muscle originates on the supraspinous fossa and inserts on the greater tubercle, initiating abduction before deltoid dominance.   92. The correct answer is B (Fibrous): fibrous joints are united by dense connective tissue and permit little to no movement, including skull sutures and the syndesmosis of the forearm. Synovial joints have a joint capsule and cavity allowing free movement. Symphysis is a subtype of cartilaginous joint, not a separate category.
  93. The correct answer is A (Fascia): fascia forms an interconnected fibrous network wrapping muscles, bones, nerves, and organs and contributes to force transmission throughout the body. Periosteum specifically covers bone surfaces. Aponeurosis is a flattened tendinous sheet connecting muscle to another structure.
  94. The correct answer is D (Referred pain): this occurs because visceral and somatic afferents converge on shared dorsal horn neurons, causing the brain to misinterpret the pain location. Neuropathic pain arises from nerve damage itself rather than convergence. Nociceptive pain is the direct detection of tissue-damaging stimuli at the actual site.
  95.   96.   100.   102.   103.   106.   107. The correct answer is C (Synarthrosis): this term describes immovable joints, exemplified by cranial sutures that fuse over time and permit essentially no motion.   108.   109. Tendon tension is instead monitored by Golgi tendon organs. Skin temperature and joint capsule pressure are detected by entirely different receptor types.
  110.   111. The correct answer is A (An increase in baseline muscle tone and guarding): sympathetic arousal prepares the body for action by heightening muscle readiness, often presenting clinically as increased tension and guarding.   112.   113.   129. The correct answer is A (The brain and spinal cord): these two structures make up the central nervous system, serving as the primary integration and control center for the body. Sympathetic, parasympathetic, somatic, and autonomic pathways are functional subdivisions rather than the central and peripheral anatomical divisions themselves.
  130.   131.   134.   139.   141.   142.   143. The correct answer is C (Suture joint): this fibrous joint subtype specifically describes the immovable interlocking connections between cranial bones. Gomphosis describes the peg-in-socket joint of a tooth in its socket, and symphysis is a cartilaginous, not fibrous, joint type.
  144. The correct answer is C (Symphysis): this joint type is united by fibrocartilage and allows slight movement, exemplified by the pubic symphysis and intervertebral discs.   145. The correct answer is C (Ball and socket joint): the rounded femoral head fits into the acetabulum, permitting flexion, extension, abduction, adduction, and rotation in multiple planes.   146. The correct answer is D (Hinge joint): the humeroulnar joint permits flexion and extension in a single plane, similar to the motion of a door hinge. Ball and socket joints instead allow multiaxial movement, as seen at the hip and shoulder. Saddle and pivot joints describe different movement patterns found at the thumb and proximal radioulnar joint respectively.
  147. The correct answer is C (Pivot joint): this joint type allows rotation of the radius around a fixed axis at the ulna, producing forearm pronation and supination. Saddle joints instead permit a different combination of movements, as seen at the thumb. Ball and socket and hinge joints describe multiaxial and single plane joints respectively, neither matching this rotational movement.
  148. The correct answer is B (Saddle joint): this joint's reciprocally curved surfaces allow the thumb's distinctive range of motion including opposition. Pivot joints instead allow rotation around a single axis, as at the proximal radioulnar joint. Hinge and ball and socket joints describe single plane and multiaxial motion respectively, neither matching the thumb's unique saddle shaped articulation.
  149.   150.   151.   152.   153. Maximal passive stretching at this stage exceeds the tissue's current tolerance and is contraindicated.
  154.   159. The correct answer is D (Slow sustained stretching combined with gentle rhythmic rocking): Slow sustained stretch and rhythmic rocking help down regulate hypertonic, spastic muscle through gradual neuromuscular inhibition, which is appropriate for pediatric spasticity.   163.   166.   167.   169. The correct answer is A (Omit heat application and use techniques that do not raise core temperature): Heat sensitivity is a well recognized issue in multiple sclerosis, known as Uhthoff's phenomenon, so heat should be avoided and techniques chosen that will not elevate core temperature. Applying heat as usual or at a higher temperature directly risks worsening her fatigue and neurological symptoms.   170. The correct answer is D (Side lying or seated position with the head kept level or slightly elevated): Since head lowered positions provoke dizziness, side lying or seated positioning that keeps the head level or elevated avoids triggering vestibular symptoms while still allowing treatment. Finishing the session before seeking help is unsafe and could be life threatening.
  249. The correct answer is B (Meniscus, a fibrocartilaginous disc that cushions and deepens the knee joint): The menisci are fibrocartilaginous discs within the knee joint that deepen the tibial plateau, improve congruency with the femoral condyles, and absorb and distribute compressive load during weight bearing. The patellar tendon connects the patella to the tibial tuberosity and does not cushion the joint surface. The popliteus is a muscle that unlocks the knee, not a shock absorbing disc. The fibular collateral ligament provides lateral joint stability rather than load distribution.
  250. The correct answer is D (Possible cancer recurrence or metastasis given the systemic red flag symptoms present): Unexplained weight loss, night sweats, and constant non-mechanical back pain in a client with a cancer history are red flag symptoms that warrant urgent referral to rule out recurrence or metastatic disease. Mechanical strain pain typically varies with position and activity, unlike this unrelenting presentation. Menopausal symptoms do not typically include unrelenting deep back pain and involuntary weight loss together. Postural fatigue would not explain the systemic symptoms of night sweats and weight loss.

Practice Test 4 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is B (Ball and socket synovial joint, permitting triaxial movement through a shallow socket): The glenohumeral joint is a classic ball and socket synovial joint, allowing triaxial movement including flexion, extension, abduction, adduction, and rotation due to the round humeral head fitting into the shallow glenoid fossa. A hinge joint permits movement in only one plane, such as the elbow. A pivot joint allows rotation around a single axis, like the proximal radioulnar joint. A plane joint permits only gliding movements between flat surfaces.
  2. The correct answer is A (Referral to a physician before any treatment given signs suggestive of a possible clot): Signs suggestive of deep vein thrombosis following prolonged immobility, including unilateral pitting edema and a positive Homan sign, are an absolute contraindication to massage until thrombosis is medically ruled out. Deep effleurage or vigorous petrissage could dislodge a clot and cause a pulmonary embolism. Passive stretching also risks mechanical disruption of a suspected thrombus and should be avoided until cleared.
  3. The correct answer is D (L5 nerve root compression, commonly affected by an L4-L5 disc herniation): A herniation at the L4-L5 level most commonly compresses the traversing L5 nerve root as it passes through the lateral recess before exiting at the level below. The L4 root typically exits above this disc level and is less commonly affected by an L4-L5 herniation. S2 involvement would suggest a much lower lesion, not this level. There is no femoral nerve root, as the femoral nerve arises from the lumbar plexus rather than being named for a spinal level.
  4. The correct answer is B (Meralgia paresthetica, compression of the lateral femoral cutaneous nerve at the hip): Burning lateral thigh pain aggravated by tight clothing or prolonged standing, without back pain or reflex involvement, is characteristic of compression of the lateral femoral cutaneous nerve known as meralgia paresthetica. L4 radiculopathy would typically involve back pain and possible reflex changes at the knee. Piriformis syndrome causes posterior buttock and leg symptoms rather than lateral thigh burning. Iliotibial band syndrome usually presents with lateral knee pain during repetitive activity, not a nerve compression pattern.
  5. The correct answer is C (Avoid direct joint manipulation and use gentle techniques away from flare sites): During an active rheumatoid flare, inflamed joints are acutely irritable and direct manipulation or aggressive technique can increase pain and inflammation, so gentle work away from the affected joints is safer. Deep friction directly on inflamed synovium would aggravate the condition. Vigorous passive range of motion during an active flare risks joint damage and increased pain. Direct heat over an acutely inflamed joint can increase swelling and discomfort rather than help.
  6. The correct answer is B (Vertebral compression fracture, collapse of a vertebral body often from weakened bone): Sudden onset thoracic pain during lifting, a palpable step deformity, and long term corticosteroid use, a known risk factor for osteoporosis, together strongly suggest a vertebral compression fracture requiring medical referral before treatment. A simple strain would not typically present with a palpable bony step deformity. Costochondritis causes localized anterior chest wall tenderness rather than a spinal deformity. A thoracic disc bulge would not produce a palpable step change in the spinous processes.
  7. The correct answer is D (Diabetic neuropathic ulcer, a painless deep wound from repeated unnoticed pressure trauma): A painless, deep plantar ulcer combined with markedly reduced sensation in a client with long standing diabetes is characteristic of a neuropathic ulcer resulting from repeated unnoticed trauma due to peripheral nerve damage. Contact dermatitis would present with an inflamed rash rather than a deep painless ulcer. Venous stasis ulcers typically occur near the medial ankle with surrounding hemosiderin staining. Arterial ischemic ulcers are usually painful and located on the toes or lateral foot with poor pulses.
  8. The correct answer is B (NSAIDs increase risk of bruising, so ice should be monitored and skin checked frequently): Regular NSAID use can impair platelet function and increase bruising tendency, so combining it with cold application warrants closer skin monitoring to catch any adverse skin response early. NSAIDs do not eliminate bruising risk, they can actually increase it. Claiming no interaction ignores the known effect on clotting and skin sensitivity. Avoiding all treatment is an overly restrictive response when a cautious, monitored approach is appropriate.
  9. The correct answer is C (Median nerve at the carpal tunnel, compressed at the wrist causing hand numbness): Nighttime numbness in the thumb, index, and middle fingers with thenar atrophy and a positive Tinel sign at the wrist are the hallmark features of median nerve compression within the carpal tunnel. Ulnar nerve compression at the elbow affects the ring and little fingers, not the thumb side. Radial nerve compression causes wrist drop and dorsal hand numbness rather than this pattern. Thoracic outlet syndrome typically produces more diffuse arm symptoms and vascular signs rather than isolated carpal tunnel findings.
  10. The correct answer is A (Peripheral arterial disease, atherosclerotic narrowing of limb arteries reducing blood flow): Exercise induced calf pain that resolves with rest, combined with cool skin, diminished pulses, and a significant smoking history, is the classic presentation of peripheral arterial disease from atherosclerotic narrowing. Venous insufficiency typically causes aching and swelling that worsens with standing, not pain relieved by rest. Spinal stenosis causes neurogenic claudication that improves with flexion rather than simple rest alone. Deep vein thrombosis presents with constant swelling and warmth rather than intermittent exertional pain.
  11. The correct answer is B (Fascial continuity within the myofascial system, transmitting tension across connected muscular regions): A cord-like band continuous with muscle fascia that transmits tension between regions reflects the interconnected nature of the myofascial system rather than a discrete anatomical structure. Hyaline cartilage lines joint surfaces and does not transmit tension along a kinetic chain in this manner. A ligament connects bone to bone at a joint rather than following a muscular fascial line. Synovial fluid lubricates joints and has no tensile or fascial transmission role.
  12. The correct answer is C (Rheumatoid arthritis, an autoimmune disease causing symmetrical inflamed joints and prolonged stiffness): Prolonged morning stiffness over an hour, symmetrical proximal interphalangeal joint swelling, ulnar deviation, and elevated rheumatoid factor are classic autoimmune features of rheumatoid arthritis. Osteoarthritis typically affects distal interphalangeal joints with shorter morning stiffness and normal rheumatoid factor. Psoriatic arthritis usually presents with skin plaques and asymmetric joint involvement including dactylitis. Gout presents as acute monoarticular attacks, most classically at the great toe, rather than symmetric chronic hand involvement.
  13. The correct answer is D (Lymphedema, chronic non-pitting limb swelling from impaired lymphatic fluid drainage): Non-pitting swelling of an entire limb with skin thickening following lymph node removal is characteristic of lymphedema resulting from impaired lymphatic drainage. Venous insufficiency typically produces pitting edema associated with varicosities rather than thickened non-pitting skin. Cellulitis presents with acute redness, warmth, and systemic signs of infection, not a chronic thickened texture. Deep vein thrombosis usually causes unilateral acute swelling with pain rather than the chronic thickened presentation described.
  14. The correct answer is C (Cervical myelopathy, spinal cord compression at the neck causing gait and hand dysfunction): Progressive hand clumsiness, radiating numbness, and gait instability together suggest spinal cord compression at the cervical level, known as cervical myelopathy, which requires urgent medical referral. Thoracic outlet syndrome typically causes arm symptoms without gait instability or myelopathic signs.
  15. The correct answer is B (Hinge joint, a joint permitting movement in only one single plane): The humeroulnar joint is a hinge joint, permitting movement in a single plane, flexion and extension, due to the trochlea fitting into the trochlear notch. A ball and socket joint like the hip allows multiaxial movement, which does not describe this articulation. A saddle joint, such as the thumb carpometacarpal joint, allows biaxial movement. A pivot joint allows rotation around one axis, as seen at the proximal radioulnar joint, not flexion and extension.
  16. The correct answer is A (Migraine headache, unilateral throbbing pain often with visual aura and nausea): Unilateral throbbing pain with visual aura, nausea, photophobia, and relief in a dark quiet environment are classic hallmark features of migraine headache. Cluster headache causes severe unilateral orbital pain with autonomic features like tearing, occurring in clusters, not monthly with aura. Tension type headache presents as bilateral pressing pain without the aura or nausea described.
  17. The correct answer is A (Septic arthritis, a joint infection causing fever, severe pain, and marked swelling): Sudden severe worsening of joint pain with fever, chills, and complete inability to bear weight represents a medical emergency suggestive of joint infection requiring immediate referral. Bursitis flares cause localized swelling and pain but not the systemic infectious signs described here.
  18. Deep tissue massage over an infected area risks spreading the infection systemically. Ice does not address an active infectious process and delays proper care.   20.   22.   31. The correct answer is C (Decline treatment and refer for medical assessment given signs suggestive of DVT or infection): the combination of recent surgery, anticoagulant use, fever, and new calf tenderness with swelling raises strong concern for either a deep vein thrombosis or a postoperative infection, both of which require medical evaluation before any massage. Treating nearby areas or the calf itself still carries risk given the uncertainty of the underlying cause. A well healed incision site does not rule out a deeper vascular or systemic complication developing elsewhere in the leg.
